## 1. spoken order with sizes (1)

```
         → Upsell card setting: Until accepted or declined
  0.0 s  Operator: “Hi, what can I get for you today?”
  4.0 s  Customer: “I want a hot dog, a large fries and a regular coke”
         → Customer session started — order heard
         → Hot key — Going large: “large” → Premium add-ons welcome
         → Added from what was said: 1× Hot Dog
         → Added from what was said: 1× Large Fries
         → Added from what was said: 1× Regular Fountain Drink
         → Upsell shown (upsize): Large Fountain Drink — Make the Fountain Drink a Large for $0.70 more
         → Upsell brain (topping, 474 ms, 705 ms round trip): Bacon Dog — Make it a Bacon Dog for $1.23 more?
 10.0 s  Cashier presses No thanks
         → Upsell declined: Large Fountain Drink (No thanks)
         → Upsell shown (brain): Bacon Dog — Make it a Bacon Dog for $1.23 more?
 16.0 s  Paid — ticket: 1× Hot Dog, 1× Fries (Large), 1× Fountain Drink (Regular)
         → Session ended — paid · 2 upsell(s) offered, 0 accepted
```

- ✅ nothing was rung up that nobody ordered, tapped or accepted

- ✅ every order with a size is on the ticket (unless taken off or swapped)

- ✅ a card answered once was not shown again (within one visit: Clear starts a new one)
- ✅ a size-up card was for an item on the ticket
- ✅ no card called a cold item hot

**How it decided** — weighing at payment:

- *Memory:* Trying to remember the customer: listening for about 2.5 s of their speech to make a voice print.
- *Health and ingredients:* Looked at calories and ingredients: Hot Dog 520 cal and Fries (Large) 1,314 cal, 1,834 cal in all, a heavy meal. Contains eggs, milk, mustard, sesame, soy and wheat. Nothing on the ticket or in what was said points to health, so indulgent add-ons are allowed. The upsell brain scores health at 0.00 on a 0 (indulgent) to 1 (health-first) scale.
- *Previous orders:* No previous orders to draw on yet: picks come from the menu's bestsellers, what fits this order and price.
- *When it pitches:* Pitching follows the conversation, with no set number: 2 shown so far, 0 accepted. Open: next is a shake (no more size-ups: one was turned down). Card setting: Until accepted or declined (the card changes only when the customer accepts or declines it (or the ticket already has what it offers); until the cashier says it, the screen asks to voice it).

Steps:

- 4.0 s · visit · New customer at the counter (order heard).
- 4.0 s · hotkey · Heard “large”: hot word “Going large”, which means not price-led; big appetite or a long day ahead. Premium add-ons welcome.
- 4.0 s · voice · Trying to remember the customer: listening for about 2.5 s of their speech to make a voice print.
- 4.0 s · order · Heard the order: rang up 1× Hot Dog from what the customer said.
- 4.0 s · order · Heard the order: rang up 1× Large Fries from what the customer said.
- 4.0 s · order · Heard the order: rang up 1× Regular Fountain Drink from what the customer said.
- 4.0 s · pick · Showing “Large Fountain Drink” (a size up). Why: make the Fountain Drink a Large for $0.70 more.
- 10.0 s · outcome · Customer turned down “Large Fountain Drink” (No thanks).
- 11.9 s · pick · Showing “Bacon Dog” [Topping] (the upsell brain's pick). Why: ladder step: topping; meal 1834 cal (heavy), health 0.00. Ladder step “topping”: ranked Bacon Dog 0.62 and Cheese Dog 0.62. No order history for this voice, so options are ranked on fit (half), bestseller (a third) and price fit (a sixth). Qwen3-14b chose from the top 3 and worded it. The model's read: “Topping for Hot Dog”
- 14.0 s · screen · Screen: Cashier listening. The order is being spoken, not browsed. So: let the heard order fill the ticket.
- 16.0 s · memory · Adding this transaction to memory (no voice print was made, so it is not linked to anyone): Hot Dog, Fries and Fountain Drink ($20.66), 2 lines of conversation, 2 upsells (0 accepted, 1 declined).
- 16.0 s · visit · Visit ended (paid): 2 upsells shown, 0 accepted.
